[Round 1 审稿请求]:
## 交叉分享：Reviewer 1 的完整审稿意见

请逐条回应以下 Reviewer 1 的审稿意见。对每一条使用 [AGREE] 同意并说明理由，或 [REBUT] 反驳并提供证据（cite 具体 Section/Table/Figure）。

---

以下是 Reviewer 1 的完整审稿原文：

### Overall: 3.0 (Findings)

### Strengths

**S1.** Layer selection sensitivity 是一个有价值的方法学发现。五指标消融清晰展示 crossing rate 如何系统性偏向 shallow layers。
**S2.** 高度透明的局限性报告。主动披露 2,240 configs、CP annotation IAA 缺失、FPR 39-52%。Bug disclosure 罕见且值得赞赏。
**S3.** 7-8B 规模结果稳健。5-seed std < 8pp，control task selectivity 12.5-19.7pp。
**S4.** 负面结果的系统性报告。RepE metric-dependent discrepancy 分析有价值。
**S5.** 多层次 robustness check。PCA capacity control、position residualization、encoder robustness、threshold sensitivity、K-ablation。

### Must-Fix 问题

**N1** [critical] "All four models pass HB" 在 QwQ-32B 上具有误导性
- QwQ-32B position-only baseline BA(0.832) > HS BA(0.796)（Table 3），HS vs position precision +4.5pp p=0.133 不显著。"All four pass HB" 是 HS vs text 比较，但 text baseline 弱于 trivial position baseline，通过 HB 的意义有限。
- 建议：调整为 "3/4 models + QwQ-32B with position caveat"，补充 residualized modality comparison。

**N2** [critical] R1-32B 统计显著性极度脆弱
- 仅 W=1 通过 HB，coverage 25%（5/20 traces）。5-seed precision std=0.128，FPR std=0.145（outlier seed FPR=0.483）。
- 建议：报告 5-seed HB passing rate，标注 R1-32B 为 marginal。

**N3** [critical] Joint (layer, W) validation 不完整
- 仅对 QwQ-32B 做了 grid search（192 configs），R1-32B 完全缺失。对以 "layer selection sensitivity" 为标题的论文，只在 1/4 模型上验证 joint stability 是严重方法学缺口。
- 建议：至少在 R1-32B 上补充 joint grid search。

### Should-Fix 问题

**W1** CP annotation IAA 未知，威胁 lead time 结论。
**W2** PCA-384d "ruling out capacity explanation" 措辞过强。
**W3** Canonical split vs 5-seed mean 存在差异。R1-8B canonical ΔPrec = +32.5pp，需确认 5-seed mean Δ 一致性。
**W4** 7 种替代方法配置空间不够充分。RepE 未尝试 iterative variant；transfer 未尝试 CCA/Procrustes。
**W5** Control task selectivity 偏低。R1-8B 12.5pp / above-chance 30pp ≈ 42% task-specific。

### 小问题
- m1: GRU 70/30 vs LR 60/20/20 标注不清
- m2: R1-32B std=0.128 应在主文突出
- m3: Bug disclosure 未交代 bug 期间其他分析
- m4: K=5 "pre-selection" 无 pre-registration 证据
- m5: Position residualization 缺 R1-32B 结果
- m6: "Encoder-conditional" framing 隐含维度是唯一变量

### Questions
Q1: 5-seed 条件下各模型 HB passing rate？
Q2: QwQ-32B position-residualized HS vs text 的 HB 结果？
Q3: CP annotation guideline 和操作化细节？
Q4: R1-32B joint (layer, W) grid search 的计算可行性？
Q5: Oracle gap 是否因 training set overfit 而被放大？

---

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The Director is asking me to cross-review Reviewer 1's comments. I need to go through each issue and provide [AGREE] or [REBUT] with evidence from the paper. Let me carefully consider each point.

Let me re-read the key sections I need to reference for this cross-review.

Let me think through each point:

**N1: "All four models pass HB" misleading for QwQ-32B**
- R1 says QwQ-32B position baseline BA(0.832) > HS BA(0.796), and HS vs position precision +4.5pp p=0.133 is not significant.


- The paper's claim about all four passing HB is specifically about HS versus TEXT, not HS versus position. R1's concern is valid though—if the position baseline outperforms the text baseline, then beating text isn't particularly meaningful. I actually raised this same issue myself, so I agree the position baseline problem is legitimate.

**N2: R1-32B statistical significance is fragile**
- I agree with this one too—I flagged it as my own N2. The paper's own data supports this concern.

**N3: Joint (layer, W) validation incomplete**
- The paper only performed joint grid search for QwQ-32B, leaving the validation approach inconsistent across models.
- For a paper focused on layer selection sensitivity, this is a reasonable criticism. I'd call it a gap that should be addressed rather than a fundamental flaw, since the five-metric ablation in Table 2 already shows the bias across all four models. The joint grid search feels more like additional confirmation than core evidence.

**W1: CP annotation IAA unknown**
- I raised this concern as well, so I agree it's worth noting.

**W2: PCA-384d "ruling out capacity explanation" too strong**
- The paper uses careful language here—"pure capacity explanation" and "capacity gap as the sole explanation"—so the qualifiers do matter. The wording isn't actually as overreaching as it might initially seem.
- On the point about PCA retaining directions of maximum variance rather than the text encoder's semantic dimensions, the paper does acknowledge this limitation explicitly in Section 4.3, so I think the framing is sufficiently cautious.

**W3: Canonical split vs 5-seed mean discrepancy**
- Looking at the R1-8B results, the canonical ΔPrec is +32.5pp, and the 5-seed mean precision for HS is 0.590±0.072 from Table K.1. The text probe data requires computing from Table 3 using the canonical precision minus ΔPrec, though the canonical split uses a specific seed configuration that may not align perfectly with the 5-seed average.
- This is a fair concern—if the canonical split happens to be the best-performing seed, that would inflate the headline results.

**W4: Alternative methods configuration space insufficient**
- For RepE, the paper tests the mean-difference direction, which is the standard approach, and the "iterative variant" isn't really a standard RepE method. For transfer learning, the paper acknowledges CCA and Procrustes as unexplored in Section 4.5, and testing 17 transfer configurations represents a reasonable exploration of the space.
- I'd push back on this one—the paper's choices seem justified and the acknowledged gaps are clearly stated.

**W5: Control task selectivity low**
- R1-8B shows 12.5pp selectivity, but R1 frames this differently: they're asking what proportion of the probe's above-chance performance is actually task-specific versus exploiting non-linguistic features. If linguistic accuracy is around 80% with 50% chance baseline, that's 30pp above chance, making the 12.5pp selectivity only about 42% task-specific. That's a legitimate concern about whether the model is really learning the linguistic property or just picking up on spurious correlations.
- I'd partially agree with this critique.

Now looking at the minor issues: the absolute selectivity does clear Hewitt & Manning's threshold, but the relative proportion is indeed lower than ideal. The GRU/LR architecture difference in m1 is mentioned in the appendix but could be more prominent. For m2, the R1-32B standard deviation should really be highlighted in the main text rather than buried in a caveat. The bug disclosure in m3 doesn't adequately explain what other analyses were affected. On m4, the K=5 pre-selection claim lacks pre-registration evidence, so it's unverifiable. And m5 points out that position residualization is missing R1-32B results.

The m6 issue is that framing the comparison as "encoder-conditional" obscures the fact that changing from 384d to 1024d actually shifts both the dimension and the encoder quality simultaneously, creating a confound.

Looking at the open questions: the 5-seed HB passing rate isn't directly reported, and establishing whether QwQ-32B shows a genuine modality advantage through position-residualized comparisons would be important. The CP annotation details need clarification, and running a joint grid search on R1-32B would strengthen the findings. The oracle gap is potentially inflated since it uses perfect training set accuracy.

Now I'm drafting the cross-review response.
```

```text
350	\section{Control Task Methodology}
351	\label{app:control}
352	
353	We follow the selectivity framework of \cite{hewitt2019control} to verify that our probes capture genuine safety-relevant structure rather than surface-level correlations.
354	
355	\paragraph{Procedure.} For each model, we randomly permute the binary labels (pre-CP vs. post-CP) across all steps while preserving the marginal label distribution. We then retrain the probe with identical architecture (L2-regularized logistic regression) and hyperparameters on these shuffled labels. Selectivity is defined as:
356	\begin{equation}
357	    \text{Selectivity} = \text{Acc}_{\text{linguistic}} - \text{Acc}_{\text{control}}
358	\end{equation}
359	where $\text{Acc}_{\text{linguistic}}$ is the probe accuracy on true labels and $\text{Acc}_{\text{control}}$ is the accuracy on permuted labels. A selectivity of zero indicates that the probe exploits only non-linguistic features (e.g., position); higher values confirm that the representation encodes task-relevant information.
360	
361	\paragraph{Results.} We repeat the control experiment 5 times (10 for OT-7B) with different random permutations and report the mean control accuracy. Selectivity scores at corrected layers are: R1-32B 19.7pp (layer 63), OT-7B 18.3pp (layer 16), QwQ-32B 14.4pp (layer 60), R1-8B 12.5pp (layer 14). All values exceed the 5pp threshold recommended by \cite{hewitt2019control}. R1-32B achieves the strongest selectivity (19.7pp) at the corrected layer, a 2.6$\times$ increase from 7.5pp at the biased layer L10. OT-7B achieves the second-highest selectivity at a mid-depth transformer layer (57\%), consistent with R1-8B's pattern at 44\% depth. On QwQ-32B, the position baseline accuracy (0.705) slightly exceeds the HS probe (0.667) at L60, consistent with the main-text finding that QwQ's HS advantage is concentrated in precision and FPR rather than balanced accuracy.
362	
363	\paragraph{Layer provenance note.} R1-8B (L14) and OT-7B (L16) are unaffected by the layer selection bias because their biased and corrected layers coincide. QwQ-32B selectivity is 14.4pp at L60 (comparable to 15.3pp at L2), confirming that safety-specific signal persists at deep layers.
364	
365	\paragraph{Position-only baseline.} As a complementary validation, we train a logistic regression classifier using only the normalized step index ($t / T$ where $T$ is the total trace length) as the input feature. This baseline tests whether temporal position alone explains probe performance. On R1-8B, the position-only baseline produces 15.8\% FPR compared to 8.1\% for the HS probe at matched detection rates. The probe's substantially lower FPR confirms that hidden state features carry information beyond what step position provides.
366	
367	
368	\section{Oracle Upper Bound Analysis}
369	\label{sec:oracle}
```

```text
60	\label{tab:hs_vs_text}
61	\end{table}
62	
63	Against the 384d baseline, HS probes significantly outperform text probes on all four models across all three metrics (\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}), though R1-8B FPR significance is fragile across seeds (2/5 uncorrected, 1/5 Bonferroni-corrected; \cref{tab:r18b_fpr_seeds}). At 7--8B, the evidence is strong: precision gains of +32.5pp (R1-8B) and +21.9pp (OT-7B), stable across seeds (std ${<}4$pp). R1-8B FPR advantage is directionally consistent across seeds (5/5) but not robust to data split (2/5 reach $p{<}0.05$ uncorrected; after Bonferroni correction for 5 seeds, 1/5 remains significant; \cref{app:multi_seed}). At 32B, both models are significant (HB corrected) but with model-specific caveats: R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ with 25\% coverage (5/20 traces; significance at this coverage means the probe fires reliably on a narrow subset rather than detecting commitment broadly), and QwQ-32B's position baseline is competitive (\cref{sec:32b-analysis}). Control task selectivity confirms genuine safety signal: 12.5--19.7pp above chance (\cref{app:control}).
64	
65	\paragraph{Capacity control.} PCA reduction of HS features to 384d (matching text encoder dimensionality, 79--92\% variance retained) preserves the precision advantage on all four models (+16.8 to +23.1pp, all $p{<}0.012$), with all four model-level precision tests passing Holm-Bonferroni. Two individual metric comparisons are non-significant: R1-8B balanced accuracy (+1.3pp, $p{=}0.731$, effect too small at $n{=}43$) and QwQ-32B FPR ($-$11.4pp, $p{=}0.038$, below HB threshold). PCA retains directions of maximum variance in hidden-state space, which need not align with the semantic dimensions captured by a trained text encoder; the control rules out a pure capacity explanation but not all encoder-quality effects. This confirms a modality effect rather than a capacity artifact.
66	
67	\paragraph{Encoder robustness.} The 1024d rows in \cref{tab:hs_vs_text} replace MiniLM (384d) with BGE-large~\citep{xiao2023cpack} (1024d, a 2.7$\times$ stronger encoder). The HS precision advantage persists directionally on all four models but narrows substantially: only OT-7B survives Holm-Bonferroni (1/4 precision tests). HS balanced accuracy advantage is also directionally positive on all four models (+8.4 to +10.9pp), with 2/4 (OT-7B, R1-32B) significant under Holm-Bonferroni; FPR advantage is significant only for OT-7B (1/4). R1-8B shows an adverse FPR direction (HS FPR 1.2pp higher than BGE, $p{=}0.564$). The advantage is encoder-conditional rather than absolute (\cref{fig:forest_precision}). Layer selection (Contribution 1) is encoder-independent, as it concerns hidden-state properties across layers rather than the text baseline.
68	
69	\paragraph{Position mediation.} Position ($t/T$) is a significant component of probe performance across all models: residualization reduces HS balanced accuracy by 12--18pp at optimal $W$ (R1-8B 16.5pp, OT-7B 12.3pp, QwQ-32B 17.7pp), but all models retain substantial non-positional signal (residualized BA 0.61--0.67, all $p{<}0.001$; \cref{app:position_residual}). Temporal windowing reduces position dependence (R1-8B: 24.3pp drop at $W{=}3$ vs.\ 16.5pp at $W{=}15$), and HS residualized probes retain more non-positional signal than text probes (+3.7 to +6.9pp BA gap).
70	
71	\paragraph{Caveats.} R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ with 25\% coverage (5/20 traces); at $W{\geq}3$, dimensionality ($51200 \times W$ for $n{=}20$) prevents significance. Sweeping regularization $C$ over $[0.01, 100]$ yields ${<}2$pp variation in balanced accuracy; the HS advantage is also stable across decision thresholds (BA variation ${<}1.5$pp across $\tau \in [0.3, 0.7]$; \cref{app:threshold_sensitivity}). QwQ-32B HS lead time is negative ($-$1.2 to +1.9 steps vs.\ text 3.7--12.3 steps): higher precision at later detection. On benign traces, probes produce 39--52\% false alarm rates, so current methods characterize the commitment phenomenon rather than constitute production-ready detectors. Per-model details in \cref{sec:32b-analysis}.
72	
73	\subsection{32B Model-Specific Analysis}\label{sec:32b-analysis}
74	
75	R1-32B and QwQ-32B share the Qwen-32B architecture (64 layers, 5120 hidden) but present opposite detection profiles (\cref{tab:32b_divergence}); training method is the likely differentiator.
76

78	\centering
79	\small
80	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
81	\toprule
82	\textbf{Model} & \textbf{Cov.} & \textbf{Lead (steps)} & \textbf{HB} & \textbf{Pos.\ margin} \\
83	\midrule
84	R1-32B & 25\% & +11.7 to +13.3 & \checkmark{} ($W{=}1$) & +14.2pp \\
85	QwQ-32B & 74\% & $-$1.2 to +1.9 & \checkmark{} (all $W$) & $-$3.6pp \\
86	\bottomrule
87	\end{tabular}
88	\caption{32B cross-model divergence. R1-32B provides limited early detection with narrow coverage; QwQ-32B provides robust significance with poor lead time. HB: \checkmark{} = model-level test passes Holm-Bonferroni ($\alpha{=}0.05$). Pos.\ margin: HS balanced accuracy minus position-only baseline.}
89	\label{tab:32b_divergence}
90	\end{table}
91	
92	R1-32B detects commitment 11--13 steps early with a +14.2pp margin over the position baseline, which confirms genuine representational signal, but covers only 25\% of traces at the single significant window ($W{=}1$). QwQ-32B achieves statistical significance at all window sizes with 74\% coverage, but HS probes fire later than text probes (negative lead time). Consistent with the universal position analysis (\cref{sec:hs-text}), QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\ 0.796). HS precision (+4.5pp, $p{=}0.133$) and FPR (+2.5pp, $p{=}0.279$) margins over the position baseline are directionally positive but not statistically significant at $n{=}19$. The divergence implies that per-model validation is essential: no single detection configuration generalizes even within the same architecture at the same scale, and a system validated on one 32B model would fail on the other.
93	
94	\subsection{Temporal Context}\label{sec:temporal}
95	
96	Temporal windowing ($W{>}1$) improves both modalities on all four models (\cref{fig:window_ablation}). Text precision improves +8 to +23pp from $W{=}1$ to $W{=}25$; HS precision improves +1.7 to +12.9pp. At 7--8B, the modality advantage (+22 to +33pp precision) exceeds the temporal improvement (+13pp). Both effects are complementary: temporal windowing ($W{=}3$--$15$) combined with principled layer selection yields the most consistent gains. Full per-model temporal ablations appear in \cref{app:temporal_details}.
97	
98	\begin{figure}[t]
99	\centering
100	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_forest_precision.pdf}
101	\caption{Forest plot of HS precision advantage over text probes. Blue: vs.\ MiniLM-384d; orange: vs.\ BGE-1024d. Solid markers: survives Holm-Bonferroni; hollow: does not. All four 384d comparisons are significant; against BGE-1024d, only OT-7B survives correction.}
102	\label{fig:forest_precision}
103	\end{figure}
104	
105	\subsection{Negative Results}\label{sec:negative}
106	
107	\begin{table}[t]
108	\centering
109	\small
110	\begin{tabular}{p{1.5cm}p{2.9cm}p{2.3cm}}
111	\toprule
112	\textbf{Method} & \textbf{Result} & \textbf{Limitation} \\
113	\midrule
114	BOCPD & 0\% det.\ on 3/4 models & Commitment gradual \\
115	CUSUM & $<$ threshold, all models & Same as BOCPD \\
116	Mahalanobis & $\Delta d \approx 0$ & Signal in minor PCs \\
117	RepE & QwQ 0.756; R1-32B 0.559$^a$ & Model-dependent \\
118	MLP & 3/4 worse than LR & Overfitting \\
119	CCS & No gain over supervised$^b$ & Alignment mismatch \\
120	Transfer & Best 59.5\% (PCA)$^c$ & Model-specific repr. \\
121	\bottomrule
122	\end{tabular}
123	\caption{Seven alternative approaches. $^a$\,RepE QwQ approaches supervised bal\_acc but precision is 0.512 vs.\ LR 0.700. $^b$\,Semi-supervised (requires CP labels). $^c$\,Best direct transfer 67.3\% but 1\% crossing rate.}
124	\label{tab:negative}
125	\end{table}
126	
127	No alternative method consistently matches supervised probing across all four models (\cref{tab:negative}). We prioritize precision over balanced accuracy as the primary evaluation criterion because in a hypothetical deployment scenario, false positives on pre-CP steps would waste intervention resources (triggering unnecessary safety responses), while missed post-CP steps merely reduce coverage without causing immediate harm. This framing is critical for interpreting RepE results below.
128	
129	\paragraph{Change-point methods.} BOCPD~\citep{adams2007bayesian} achieves 0\% detection on 3/4 models because safety commitment is gradual rather than abrupt: the probe score sequence transitions smoothly over 5--15 steps without the discontinuity that BOCPD's hazard function requires. On R1-8B (the sole model with non-zero detection), the best BOCPD configuration ($\lambda{=}10$) achieves 93\% detection rate but with lower lead time (+3.5 steps) than simple thresholding (+4.9 steps). CUSUM similarly fails because cumulative evidence never exceeds the decision boundary on smooth transitions. The gradual nature of commitment is itself an empirical finding: it rules out the entire class of abrupt-change detectors and motivates the supervised probe approach that explicitly learns the transition boundary.
130	
131	\paragraph{RepE inconsistency.} RepE computes the class-conditioned mean difference as a fixed direction (analytic), while LR optimizes cross-entropy to learn the classification boundary (iterative). Both require the same labeled data; the key distinction is parameterization, not supervision level. This difference is consequential: RepE mean-difference directions~\citep{zou2023representation} illustrate the metric-dependent nature of method comparison. On QwQ-32B, RepE achieves 0.756 balanced accuracy (comparable to supervised LR at 0.778), but its precision is 0.512 vs.\ LR 0.700 and its FPR is 0.309 vs.\ 0.120. The mean-difference direction captures enough signal to classify steps above chance, but fires indiscriminately on pre-CP steps, producing 2.6$\times$ more false alarms than supervised probes. On R1-32B, RepE nearly fails entirely (0.559 balanced accuracy). The inconsistency across two models sharing the same architecture confirms that a single linear direction computed from class means does not reliably capture safety commitment structure.
132	
133	Cross-model transfer produces no practical utility across 17 configurations; stronger alignment methods (CCA, model stitching~\cite{kornblith2019cka,bansal2021stitching}) remain unexplored. Detailed per-method analysis in \cref{app:negative_details}.
134
```

以下是我对 Reviewer 1 每一条意见的逐条回应。

---

## Must-Fix 问题

**[AGREE] R1-N1: "All four models pass HB" 在 QwQ-32B 上具有误导性**

同意这是一个重要问题。我在自己的审稿中也独立提出了类似关注（我的 W2）。QwQ-32B 的 position baseline BA(0.832) > HS BA(0.796)（Table 5, Section 4.4），且 HS vs position 的 precision (+4.5pp, p=0.133) 和 FPR (+2.5pp, p=0.279) 均不显著。这意味着论文的 "all four pass HB" 实际上是在说 HS 击败了一个比 trivial t/T baseline 更弱的 text probe——这种胜利的信息量有限。R1 建议调整为 "3/4 models + QwQ-32B with position caveat" 是合理的。补充 position-residualized 的 HS vs text HB 比较（R1 的 Q2）将更有说服力。

**[AGREE] R1-N2: R1-32B 统计显著性极度脆弱**

完全同意。我在自己的 N2 中也将此列为 critical。补充几点：(1) R1-32B W=1 时 coverage 25% 意味着 probe 仅在 5/20 traces 上触发，论文自身也承认 "significance at this coverage means the probe fires reliably on a narrow subset rather than detecting commitment broadly"（Section 4.3 L63）；(2) Appendix K Table K.3 显示 seed 0 的 FPR=0.483，一个 outlier 即可使 metric 翻倍。R1 建议报告 5-seed HB passing rate 是必要的——如果 5-seed 中仅 2-3 次通过 HB，"all four pass" 的 claim 就站不住。

**[REBUT] R1-N3: Joint (layer, W) validation 不完整——应为 Should-Fix 而非 Must-Fix**

我认为 R1 对这一问题的严重性判断偏高。理由：

1. 论文的 Contribution 1（层选择敏感性）的核心证据来自五指标消融（Table 2, Figure 1），这在全部四个模型上都已完成。Joint (layer, W) grid search 是对已选层的稳定性的二次验证，不是核心论证的基础。
2. QwQ-32B 和 R1-32B 共享相同架构（Qwen-32B, 64 layers, 5120 hidden），QwQ-32B 的 joint grid search 结果（Table A.1: L60 在 W=3-5 near-optimal）对 R1-32B 有一定可迁移性。
3. R1-32B 的根本问题是样本量（n=20），而非 joint validation 的缺失。即使补充了 joint grid search，n=20 的限制仍然使任何统计结论都很脆弱。

不过我同意这是一个 **should-fix** 级别的方法学缺口，论文至少应解释为什么只在 QwQ-32B 上做了 joint grid search（计算限制？还是遗漏？），并讨论 R1-32B 的可迁移性假设。

## Should-Fix 问题

**[AGREE] R1-W1: CP annotation IAA 未知，威胁 lead time 结论**

同意。我在自己的 W3 中也提出了这一点。论文 Limitations Section 承认"We do not independently evaluate inter-annotator agreement"，并指出 "If CP annotation error is ≥2 steps, the reported lead times (+4.5 to +13 steps) may partially fall within annotation noise"。对于以 step-level 时间分辨率为核心评估维度的论文，IAA 的缺失确实是一个非平凡的威胁。

**[REBUT] R1-W2: PCA-384d "ruling out capacity explanation" 措辞过强**

部分不同意。论文的实际措辞是 "ruling out **a pure** capacity explanation"（Abstract L3）和 "ruling out capacity gap **as the sole** explanation"（Section 4.3 L65）。"Pure" 和 "sole" 是重要的限定词。此外，Section 4.3 明确写道："PCA retains directions of maximum variance in hidden-state space, which need not align with the semantic dimensions captured by a trained text encoder; the control rules out a pure capacity explanation **but not all encoder-quality effects**"。这已经是相当谨慎的表述。

R1 的担忧有道理——PCA-384d 和 trained-384d encoder 确实不是等价的（前者保留方差最大方向，后者学习语义维度），所以这个 control 不完美。但论文的措辞已经包含了这个 caveat，不需要进一步弱化。我建议的改进是：如果空间允许，加一句明确说明 PCA-384d 和 MiniLM-384d 在信息保留上的结构性差异，使 "pure capacity" 的限定更易理解。

**[AGREE] R1-W3: Canonical split vs 5-seed mean 存在差异**

同意这是一个值得关注的问题。Table 3 报告的 canonical ΔPrec = +32.5pp（R1-8B），但 Appendix K Table K.1 的 5-seed HS precision mean 为 0.590±0.072。如果 text probe 同样有跨 seed 变异，5-seed 的 mean ΔPrec 可能显著低于 32.5pp。论文在 Table 3 caption 中注明 "Values from canonical split; 5-seed mean±std in App K"，但未直接报告 5-seed mean Δ。这让读者无法判断 headline 数字是否代表了 typical case 还是 best case。至少应在主文或 appendix 中报告 5-seed mean ΔPrec。

**[REBUT] R1-W4: 7 种替代方法配置空间不够充分**

部分不同意。

关于 RepE：标准 RepE（Zou et al. 2023）就是 mean-difference direction，这是原文定义的方法。R1 提到的 "iterative variant" 不是 RepE 的标准变体——如果指的是 iterative refinement 或 contrastive learning，那属于不同方法族。论文已经解释了 RepE 失败的结构性原因："The mean-difference direction captures enough signal to classify steps above chance, but fires indiscriminately on pre-CP steps"（Section 4.5 L131）。这是方法论层面的 limitation，不太可能通过迭代变体解决。

关于 Transfer：论文明确承认 "stronger alignment methods (CCA, model stitching) remain unexplored"（Section 4.5 L133）。17 种 PCA 配置全部失败，且在 maximally favorable 条件下（PCA fitted on full data including test），这强化了 negative conclusion 的可信度。不过 R1 的建议原则上是对的——CCA/Procrustes 至少值得尝试一两个配置。我将此定位为 minor improvement 而非 fundamental gap。

**[AGREE] R1-W5: Control task selectivity 偏低**

部分同意 R1 的分析框架。R1-8B selectivity 12.5pp 确实通过了 Hewitt & Manning 2019 的 5pp 阈值（Appendix E），但 R1 的相对比例计算也有道理：如果 linguistic accuracy ≈ 80%，chance ≈ 50%，则 above-chance = 30pp，selectivity/above-chance = 42%，意味着 58% 的 above-chance performance 来自非 task-specific features（如 position）。不过需要注意：(1) 论文已通过 position residualization 独立验证了非位置信号的存在（Section 4.3 L69, Appendix G）；(2) R1-32B 的 selectivity 达到 19.7pp，是最高的。控制任务和 position residualization 提供了互补的证据，单独看任何一个都不够强，但组合起来构成了合理的论证。

## 小问题

**[AGREE] R1-m1**: GRU 70/30 vs LR 60/20/20 在主文确实容易引起混淆。Appendix D 有详细说明，但 Section 3.2 首次提及 GRU 时应明确标注 split 差异。

**[AGREE] R1-m2**: R1-32B precision std=0.128 在主文 Section 4.3 caveats 段有提及（L71 附近 Table 3 caption），但被夹在长段落中不够突出。应在 Table 3 中用脚注或主文用粗体/下划线标记。

**[AGREE] R1-m3**: Bug disclosure（Appendix H）交代了 bug 本身和修正方式，但未说明 bug 存在期间是否有其他分析基于错误数据做出了设计决策（如层选择策略的初始选择）。虽然论文声称 "All numbers reported in this paper use the corrected pipeline"，但研究过程中的 downstream decisions 可能已受到 contamination。

**[AGREE] R1-m4**: K=5 的 "pre-selection" claim（Section 3.4）在无 pre-registration 的情况下不可验证。论文的 K-ablation（Appendix F）展示了 K∈{3,5,7} 的平滑过渡，这部分缓解了 post-hoc selection 的担忧，但 "pre-selection" 的措辞仍应改为更谨慎的表述。

**[AGREE] R1-m5**: Position residualization（Appendix G, Table G.1）确实缺少 R1-32B 结果。Table G.1 包含 R1-8B（两个 W）、OT-7B、QwQ-32B，但 R1-32B 完全缺失。这与论文 "all four models" 的叙事框架不一致。

**[AGREE] R1-m6**: "Encoder-conditional" 的 framing 暗示维度是 384d vs 1024d 对比中的主要变量，但实际上 MiniLM-L6-v2 → BGE-large-en-v1.5 的切换同时改变了（1）输出维度、（2）模型容量/参数量、（3）训练数据和目标。论文在 Section 4.3 L67 中注明了 BGE 是 "a 2.7× stronger encoder"，但未明确拆解维度 vs 编码质量的贡献。这与我自己审稿中 N1 的关注点一致。

## Questions

**[AGREE] R1-Q1**: 5-seed 条件下各模型 HB passing rate 是一个关键缺失信息。如果 R1-32B 在 5 seed 中仅 2/5 通过 HB，"all four pass" 的 claim 需要根本性修改。

**[AGREE] R1-Q2**: QwQ-32B position-residualized HS vs text 的 HB 结果是验证 genuine modality advantage 的关键实验。当前论文仅报告了 residualized BA（Table G.1），未报告 residualized modality comparison 的 bootstrap test。

**[AGREE] R1-Q3**: CP annotation guideline 和操作化细节对于评估 lead time 结论的可靠性至关重要。论文依赖 HarmThoughts 的 methodology 但未独立验证。

**[AGREE] R1-Q4**: R1-32B joint grid search 的计算可行性应该很高——64 layers × 3 windows × logistic regression 在单 GPU 上应该在小时级别。如果可行但未做，这是遗漏；如果有技术障碍，应说明。

**[REBUT] R1-Q5**: Oracle gap 因 training set overfit 被放大的可能性较低。Appendix F 描述 oracle 的计算方式是 "the earliest step at which the ground-truth CP could theoretically be predicted from hidden-state dynamics"（Section F L371-372），使用的是 "linearly separable from pre-CP states with perfect accuracy on the **training set**"。这确实在训练集上定义了上界，但 oracle gap 的意义是展示 passive probing 和理论上限之间的距离（85.5%），即使 oracle 被高估 10-15%，gap 仍然巨大（>70%），核心结论不变。

---
